♜ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♚ ⭘ (Rank 3: a3, g3)
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♔ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ♛ (Rank 2: d2, h2)
⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ (Rank 1)

Wait, why are there only 3 legal moves?
If the king is at d2 and the queen is at h2, the king could potentially move to e3, d3, c3, c2, e2, c1, d1, e1.
But the rook is at a3.
- c3: Attacked by rook at a3.
- d3: Attacked by rook at a3 (maybe? no, rook is at a3, king at d3... wait, a3 is a square, the rook attacks the 3rd rank). Yes, the rook at a3 attacks the whole 3rd rank.
- e3: Attacked by rook at a3.
- c2: Attacked by queen at h2.
- e2: Attacked by queen at h2.
- d2: Current position (check).
- c1: Safe.
- d1: Safe.
- e1: Safe.

So the legal moves are indeed d2c1, d2d1, d2e1.

Which move is better?
If Kd1, it's a central position.
If Kc1, it's in the corner.
If Ke1, it's towards the other side.

Looking at the black pieces, the rook is at a3 and the queen is at h2.
The king at g3 is close.
If the white king moves to c1, it's further from the black king.
If the white king moves to e1, it's closer to the black king.

Let's choose d2d1 as a solid central move for the king to avoid getting trapped in the corner.
